import pathlib, re, subprocess

def run(*args):
    result = subprocess.run(args, text=True, capture_output=True, check=True)
    return result.stdout.splitlines()

containers = [n for n in run("docker", "ps", "-a", "--format", "{{.Names}}") if n.startswith("architect-")]
taps = [n for n in run("brew", "tap") if n.startswith("architect-e2e-")]
formulas = [n for n in run("brew", "list", "--formula") if n.startswith("acme-")]
ports = {4890 + check for check in range(1, 9)} | {8900 + 10 * check + delta for check in range(1, 9) for delta in range(7)}
listeners = subprocess.run(["lsof", "-nP", "-iTCP", "-sTCP:LISTEN", "-Fn"], text=True, capture_output=True)
assert listeners.returncode in (0, 1), listeners.stderr
used = {int(line.rsplit(":", 1)[1]) for line in listeners.stdout.splitlines() if line.startswith("n") and re.search(r":\d+$", line)}
root = str(pathlib.Path.cwd())
processes = []
for line in run("ps", "-axo", "pid=,comm=,args="):
    parts = line.strip().split(None, 2)
    if len(parts) != 3:
        continue
    pid, command, args = parts
    if pathlib.Path(command).name in ("node", "workerd", "wrangler") and root in args and re.search(r"wrangler|workerd|vitest", args):
        processes.append(pid)
for label, left in [("Architect containers", containers), ("gate taps", taps), ("gate formulas", formulas), ("exact gate port listeners", sorted(ports & used)), ("own runtime processes", processes)]:
    print(f"{label}: {len(left)}")
    assert not left, f"{label} remain"
print("Cleanup passed; unrelated listeners were left untouched.")

Stage 3 · shipping on local worlds (Gate 2)

L2.10 The portal: the wire projection, the snapshot over the logbook, and a plain read-only face on each environment's Worker unverified

Every environment's Worker now answers /portal (HTML) and /_portal/v1/* (JSON) from the one bundle of a revision. generated/portal/blueprint.json is the portal's wire: schemaVersion 1, the model, the generated docs, each operation's proof dependencies and a sha256 digest, deterministic (no clock, no revision). @architect/portal rejects an unknown schema or a digest mismatch, reads the logbook's open routes, and judges every operation in every environment: recorded-pass, failed (the latest failure wins), stale (naming the changed declarations and both hashes), unproven, or unavailable when the logbook is down, slow, inconsistent or lacks the model. Only a recorded pass is a tick. env up gives the Worker the logbook's address; prove --env test now keeps the revision's model in the logbook, which the portal judges verdicts against.

Done whenOn the real logbook (workerd under wrangler) holding verdicts signed by a real OIDC server in Docker, over models the real compiler wrote: spend shows test ✔ staging ✔ production ✔; a budget edit makes spend stale in staging and production and no other operation; the latest failure wins and a later pass replaces it; a pull request's signed verdict never fills the matrix; a verdict whose revision has no model, a logbook that stops, and a ledger that will not hold still each show nothing green. On a copy of acme under git, staging's and production's own workerd serve /portal from the built bundle with the data routes, the old bundle still stale-judged against the newer test revision, the logbook's address in no page, env down leaving nothing running. The portal's JSON dependency adapter equals Compare.dependsOn on acme, acme with a list read, the compiler's recorded snapshot of acme and the starter; the portal is imported by generated/portal/main.ts only. The author's check 8 passes its first portal assertion and stops at architect watch (L2.09); its portal steps pass in the real flow with the watch steps taken out. Lint, typecheck, sync check, every project's tests, acme's and the starter's prove, and tools/e2e/starter.sh pass.

=============== pooled-socket-note.txt
A hazard in the author's check 08 (and any check shaped like it), found while running its portal half. Not the portal's: any route shows it.

The check calls `portal(url)` (a `fetch`) at its start, then runs only `spawnSync` steps (seed, replay, watch, build, promote, prove), which block the
Node event loop, and calls `portal(url)` again at its end. `promote --to staging` restarts staging in between: the old workerd dies, a new one listens on
the same port. Node's fetch (undici) kept the first request's connection in its pool, and with the loop blocked it never saw the server close it, so the
second fetch is written to a dead socket: `TypeError: fetch failed`, cause `write EPIPE` or `read ECONNRESET`.

Reproduced with the plain `/_architect/bundle` and with `/portal`, on a copy of acme: fetch, `env down`, `env up` on the same port, no async wait, fetch again:

  FIRST 200 {"content-encoding":"gzip","content-type":"application/json","transfer-encoding":"chunked"}
  SECOND (pooled) FAILED TypeError: fetch failed Error: read ECONNRESET
  THIRD (connection: close) ok 200

With an async wait of 8 s between (the loop free to see the close) the pooled fetch works. A Worker cannot prevent it: a `Connection: close` header on its response
is dropped by workerd (the first response's headers show none). The cure is in the check: `fetch(url, { headers: { connection: "close" } })`, or a dispatcher
without keep-alive. The private copy of the portal half used under `tmp/` does the first.

=============== promote straight after build, as the author wrote it (captured from the real flow before the private copy proved the revision in test first)

Stage 3 · shipping on local worlds (Gate 2)

L2.09 watch: the evals on live telemetry, and drift as a task unverified

architect watch --env <name> [--source file|axiom] [--since 1h]: the evals telemetry alone can answer (answer shape, declared errors, steps, performance, explainability; not coverage) over an environment's recorded traffic, read through the TraceSource seam (window) from the Collector's files or from Axiom's query API (APL on <group>-traces, query token read by name). It prints each operation's p95 against its budget, the refusals by name and code, the defects and the drift; an operation over budget on at least 20 calls is drift and becomes a task file under .architect/tasks/. Prove's own calls are told apart by their caller span id and left out. tools/watch/replay.sh drives staging traffic.

Done whenOn a real staging world, 30 replayed spends and 3 refusals print allowance.spend p95 within its 150ms budget, AllowanceExceeded 3 and drift none; after the author's slow-spend fixture is built, proved in test, promoted and replayed, the same line is over budget with a mark of fail and drift allowance.spend, with its task written. Over Axiom (capxul-test, live) the same evals read what prove left there.

Stage 3 · shipping on local worlds (Gate 2)

L2.08 Remote MCP with OAuth, and the world's front door closed unverified

The generated server serves /mcp: one MCP tool per operation and read, built on the MCP SDK's stateless streamable HTTP server, its input schema from the declaration, only public fields in an input or an answer (a private field only to a caller who holds its role), refusals as tool errors with their code, and the API's own role check. OAuth is Better Auth's oauth-provider on the project's AuthD1, with dynamic client registration capped and the consent given headless. /_world/* now answers 404 in every environment that does not host worlds, and staging's proof reads its own database instead of the door.

Done whenAn MCP client completes OAuth as ada and calls allowance.get for the typed answer; bob is refused [AR-001] NotAllowed; a declared refusal carries its code; no secret is an input or an answer. Staging and production answer 404 on /_world/cast and the facts, while the test environment's door stays open.

L2.07 Real sign-in: device login for the CLI (the local reading) done

Better Auth 1.7.15's deviceAuthorization is in the server's auth (client acme-cli only, table auth_device_code, a 5 second polling interval), and the generated server mounts Better Auth's routes at /api/auth. The generated CLI's session sign-in --env <env> runs the device flow: it prints the code and where to approve it, polls with an Effect Schedule that backs off on slow_down, keeps the session in the config folder (ACME_CONFIG_DIR) and prints signed in. session show --env <env> uses the kept session and prints the person. The real staging sign-in is the cloud pass.

Done whenOn a real local world, the generated CLI signs in by device code; ada claims and approves the code with her own session and no browser, and session show prints her. Bob approving ada's claimed code is a 403, another client id is refused, a poll before the interval is slow_down, a code is single-use, and no token or password is printed.

L2.06 architect publish: the SDK and CLI as npm packages, a single executable and a Homebrew formula unverified

architect publish --dry-run | --local turns the generated SDK and CLI into @acme/sdk and @acme/cli at the version architect version --next says. Each is one esbuild bundle with its dependencies as installed (the SDK with type declarations from the project's tsc), sent to the registry in ARCHITECT_REGISTRY under a dist-tag: a prerelease always goes under next and never takes latest from a release. The CLI is also built as a Node 24.22.2 single executable for this machine (macOS arm64 or Linux x64), whose tarball sha256 the formula pins, committed to a local tap repository by file:// URL. promote publishes: staging the prerelease <next>-next.<n> before its verdicts line, production the release after released <version>. npmjs.org and GitHub releases print skipped (cloud pass).

Done whenThrough the command on a copy of acme under git, with a real logbook, OIDC server and Verdaccio 6.11.1 in Docker and real Homebrew: --dry-run lists exactly the files, binary and formula that would go out and leaves the registry and tap empty; --local puts @acme/sdk@1.1.0 and @acme/cli@1.1.0 on the registry with latest on them, and a fresh folder installs them, imports the SDK, type-checks against its declarations and runs the CLI's bin, which prints acme 1.1.0; a re-run leaves what is there alone; prereleases are numbered one past the last under next and a prerelease after a release never moves latest; brew install of the formula by full name runs, prints acme 1.1.0, passes brew test and prints ok from acme system health against a running world, then uninstall and untap. The author's check 5 passes (the SDK installs at exactly version --next, and brew install runs acme --version printing it).

not export (`ListItem`, `ListPayload`, `Page`; TS2883); they are exported now (D3 #113), and the last run is below.

Stage 3 · shipping on local worlds (Gate 2)

L2.05 architect promote: staging proved where it runs, production by what staging proved unverified

architect promote --to staging|production moves the exact bundle architect build kept, never a rebuild. It refuses a dirty tree, an unbuilt or altered bundle and a revision without main verdicts (test for staging, staging for production, every operation), naming what is missing, and starts nothing for a refusal. It then starts or restarts the target on that bundle and checks /_architect/bundle. Staging is proved where it runs (journeys with people signing up through Better Auth, evals from staging's own collector) and posts environment staging verdicts; production records the release through Releases.record and carries staging's verdicts to production. Verdicts and Releases now share one token source.

Done whenOn a copy of acme under git with the real logbook, issuer, collectors and three environments: a revision with no test verdicts, a pull request's verdicts, and a failing and a missing one are each refused with the operations named; a dirty tree and an unbuilt bundle are refused; production is refused without staging verdicts; after prove --env test, staging takes the revision and posts fifteen passing verdicts; production takes what staging proved, test, staging and production report the same revision and sha256, release 1.0.0 is recorded and promoting again records nothing. Author's checks 01, 02, 03 and 06 pass; check 04 passes except for its missing prove --env test and the registry assertions (L2.06).

Stage 3 · shipping on local worlds (Gate 2)

L2.03 The logbook and signed verdicts done

packages/logbook is a worker on local workerd under wrangler dev: a Durable Object holds verdicts and releases, R2 holds each revision's model. POST /verdicts takes a Bearer OIDC token, verified with jose against the one issuer the environment names (RS256 only, audience, repository, ref patterns, workflow and job_workflow_ref), and stores the ref and event with the verdict. Reads are open. Beyond the verdicts it keeps a revision's model, the latest verdict per operation and environment, and the last production release, for versions and staleness in L2.04 and promotion in L2.05. The test runs the real worker against a real OIDC server (mock-oauth2-server, by digest, in Docker).

Done whenA trusted token's verdict is accepted and read back with its ref and event. A forged token, another issuer's token, a wrong branch, repository, workflow or audience, no token and a non-JWT are each 401 and store nothing. State, model and release survive a restart. Nothing is left running.

Stage 3 · shipping on local worlds (Gate 2)

L2.02 Test worlds, and the logbook as an environment unverified

architect prove --env test makes world test-<id> inside the long-lived test environment (started if down), with the blueprint's lifetime or ARCHITECT_LIFETIME. The world is its own Durable Object with an alarm at its expiry; its data is whatever is above the high-water mark of every table, so destroying it (alarm, reap or explicit) deletes exactly that. The journeys and evals run against it, one verdict per operation is posted to the logbook with an OIDC token (the local issuer, or the CI job's own) that is never printed, and the world is destroyed. architect worlds reap starts the environment if it is down, destroys every world past its lifetime and stops it again; env status test adds worlds <n>; env up logbook runs the logbook worker through Logbook.launch. The test environment has a collector of its own, removed by env down.

Done whenprove --env test posts fifteen passing verdicts that the logbook reads back with their ref, leaves no world and no row, and a run that fails to record its verdicts still destroys its world. A run killed with SIGKILL and a 20 s lifetime leaves a world that reap leaves alone within its lifetime and removes after it; the second reap says 0. The author's checks 01 and 02 pass. Nothing is left running. The tests were written alongside the build, not before it.

Stage 3 · ship it across local worlds (Gate 2)

L2.01 Environments and one build per revision (local reading) done

architect build bundles the revision's Worker once with wrangler's dry run, with no credentials, and prints built <revision> sha256:<hash>; the same revision hashes the same. architect env up, down and status run test, staging and production as long-lived wrangler dev processes on that exact file, each with its own D1 state, port, inspector port and secrets file, and every one answers GET /_architect/bundle with the revision and hash it runs. The declarations (Blueprint.environment, config and secret, Identity, Cloud, Deploy) are read into the model by file path, and acme and the starter carry the four environment files. The Alchemy stack and architect deploy wait for the cloud pass.

 ✓ layer1/06-check-and-the-authoring-loop.test.ts > check 6: architect add, generate and check build the allowance on the starter > check/refusal-without-log/spend.ts: check exits 1 naming implementation/handlers/allowance/spend.ts and refusal-has-log 1770ms
stdout | layer1/04-agent-writes-a-handler.test.ts > check 4: an agent writes allowance.spend from its brief alone > the written handler passes prove: spend's 9 evals and the journeys that call it
agent session: success, 18 turns, 24 s, $0.05
I wrote `implementation/handlers/allowance/spend.ts`, and `architect check` passes (types, rules and drift). I haven't run `architect prove --env local`, because I have no tool for it. So the 9 evals and the three journeys are untested.

The handler works like this:
- **Load:** it loads the allowance through `steps.load(...)` and works out what is left (`limit - spent`).
- **Refusal:** if the amount is more than what is left, it logs why with `Effect.logInfo` and refuses with `AllowanceExceeded`, carrying the remaining amount.
- **Record:** otherwise it writes the new `spent` total through `steps.record(...)`.
- **Move:** it then calls `move("exhausted")` if nothing is left, or `move("active")` if not.
- **Answer:** it returns `{ remaining }`.

I didn't handle `AllowanceNotActive` or `NotAllowed`; the runtime refuses those before the handler runs.

I couldn't find documentation for the `Records` API. I assumed `records.update(Allowance, id, { spent })` exists, based on how `grant.ts` and `accept.ts` use `records.insert` and `records.find`. Check passed with it, so it type-checks. Whether it behaves correctly is what the prove run will show.

L1.33 architect brief done

architect brief gives an agent everything it needs to write one handler, and fences it in. For allowance.spend it names the one file to write, the declaration and generated pages to read, the two steps to run, the move and its outcomes, the one refusal to give with its exit code, and the two the runtime gives instead. It lists every folder not to touch, journeys included, and says what must pass: prove with spend's nine evals and the three journeys that call it, and check.

Done whenarchitect brief allowance.spend prints the declaration to read, the files it must not touch (including blueprint/journeys/**), and the evals that must pass.
CI success · commit bb7e816 · 2 verified captures · 0 unverified records
passed The brief for allowance.spend, from acme. exit 0 · 0.5s · 2026-11-16T18:45:50Z
$ node packages/cli/src/main.ts brief allowance.spend --root examples/acme
brief allowance.spend

Write   implementation/handlers/allowance/spend.ts, and nothing else.

Read
  blueprint/things/allowance/verbs/spend.ts  the declaration: payload, answer, move, steps, errors
  generated/api/src/allowance/spend.handler.ts  what the handler is given and may return
  generated/docs/allowance/spend.md  the same, in words, with every refusal
  generated/docs/allowance/index.md  the allowance: fields, machine, roles

Do
  run step allowance.spend.load (database) through steps.load(...)
  run step allowance.spend.record (database) through steps.record(...)
  make the move with move("active" | "exhausted"): spend, emitting AllowanceSpent
  refuse with AllowanceExceeded (exit 41), each after an Effect.logInfo saying why
  never refuse AllowanceNotActive or NotAllowed yourself: the runtime does, before the handler runs

Don't touch
  blueprint/**
  blueprint/journeys/**
  blueprint/starting-worlds/**
  journeys/**
  generated/**
  test/**

  and the journeys that call it: journeys/revoke-then-spend.ts, journeys/spend-twice-with-one-request.ts, journeys/spend-within-allowance.ts
  architect check passes

passed The generated page for allowance.spend. exit 0 · 0.0s · 2026-11-16T18:44:20Z
$ cat examples/acme/generated/docs/allowance/spend.md
<!-- Generated from the blueprint. Do not edit. -->
# allowance.spend

Spend from what is left of this month's allowance.

**Who may call:** the grantee of the allowance.

**Idempotent by request:** send `idempotency-key`; the same key twice is one call.

**Budget:** p95 150ms.

## Payload

| field | value |
|---|---|
| allowance | allowance.id |
| amount | Amount |
| memo | Memo |

## Answer

| field | value |
|---|---|
| remaining | Amount |

## Move

`spend`: from active to active or exhausted, emitting AllowanceSpent.

## Steps

- `allowance.spend.load` (database)
- `allowance.spend.record` (database)

## Refusals

| refusal | exit | when |
|---|---|---|
| AllowanceExceeded | 41 | declared in errors.ts, carrying remaining |
| AllowanceNotActive | 40 | the allowance is in a state spend can't start from; carries `state` |
| NotAllowed | 3 | the caller isn't the grantee of the allowance; the handler never runs |

## How to call

```sh
acme allowance spend --allowance <allowance> --amount <amount> --memo <memo>
```

HTTP: `POST /allowance/spend` with the payload as JSON. SDK: `Sdk.allowance.spend(payload)`.

Stage 2 · the allowance's whole life (Gate 1)

L1.31 architect generate, and handler scaffolds done

architect generate brings a project's generated code in line with its blueprint, the same as nx sync, and tells you what it changed. Each operation that has no blanks left gets a handler scaffold, written once and yours from then on. An operation with a blank gets nothing yet; fill it in and the next run scaffolds it. Run it twice and the second run says nothing to change.

Done whenFrom acme's blueprint with no implementation, generate creates the four operation scaffolds and none for a blank operation; filled in, that one is scaffolded; a further run changes nothing.

// Scaffolded from blueprint/things/allowance/verbs/spend.ts. Yours from now on.
import { Effect } from "effect";
import { Handlers } from "@architect/target-effect";
import type { Handler } from "../../../generated/api/src/allowance/spend.handler.ts";

/** Spend from what is left of this month's allowance. */
const spend: Handler = (_payload, _context) =>
  Effect.gen(function* () {
    // Payload: allowance, amount, memo.
    // Answer: remaining.
    // Steps, each its own span: steps.load(...), steps.record(...).
    // May refuse with AllowanceExceeded.
    return yield* Handlers.blank("this handler is not written yet");
  });

export default spend;
Stage 2 · the allowance's whole life (Gate 1)

L1.30 Blanks, and architect add done

You can now start a declaration before you know all of it. architect add writes a thing, an operation, a read or a journey, and every part you still have to decide is a blank that says what belongs there. Blanks compile. architect check names each one with its file and line, and fails until they're filled. The compiler keeps track of them too: an operation with a blank isn't generated yet. add never overwrites anything, and --dry-run shows what it would write.

Done whenIn a fresh project, architect add thing allowance writes five files and a values file; tsc compiles them; architect check prints allowance has 3 blanks with the file and line of each.

L1.25 Nine evals for every operation done

prove now prints nine evals for every operation, the way the docs show it: answer shape, declared errors, machine path, events, who may call, steps, performance, coverage and explainability, each with its p50, p95 and budget. Coverage became each operation's own eval: it fails an operation no journey calls, a move no journey makes, an outcome never reached or a declared error never given. One line then counts the whole run: three moves, three outcomes, both declared refusals, and four of seven runtime refusals. Every planted fixture still fails its own eval.

Stage 2 · the allowance's whole life (Gate 1)

L1.21 Errors and the logs that decide them done

A refusal now carries the log line that decided it. While the handler runs, a second logger sits beside the project's and remembers the last thing logged; when the handler refuses, that line goes on the operation's span as refusal.log. The handler's errors are held to what its operation declares by the generated types: the journey author's fixture, a spend that fails with an error spend never declared, fails to compile, and acme as written compiles. The types are structural, so two errors with the same fields would slip past; a lint rule in L1.40 closes that.

Done whenA type test compiles a copy of acme with the planted handler and tsc rejects it at spend.ts naming AllowanceLimitTooHigh; the clean copy compiles. A refusal span carries its deciding log.

prove ✔
Stage 2 · the allowance's whole life (Gate 1)

L1.20 Machines and moves, with reads and idempotency done

The allowance now lives its whole life: granted, spent, exhausted and revoked. A call commits once. The handler's writes are held back, then the move, its new state and its event are committed together, as one D1 batch or inside the instance's own Durable Object. Every update is guarded by the version it read, so fifty spends racing on one allowance all land and none is lost, on D1 and on an actor. A revoked allowance refuses a spend before the handler runs. allowance.get is answered with no handler at all, and spending twice with one request key spends once. prove passes on acme's six journeys, written by a separate author.

Done whenThe race test passes for both owners in workerd and fails when the version guard is removed. architect prove passes on acme with five operations, three moves and every eval.

Stage 1 · the skeleton, with every eval

L1.15 prove, with a real collector and all nine evals done

architect prove now judges the code for real. It starts a real OpenTelemetry collector in Docker and a fresh world on workerd. It runs the journey: Ada founds an organisation and grants Olga 500, Bob is refused, and a limit over the cap is refused. Then it reads every call's spans and logs back from the collector and checks all nine evals: answer shape, declared errors, machine path, events, who may call, steps, performance, coverage and explainability. Acme passes in about six seconds and nothing is left running. Each of the eight broken fixtures, planted by a separate author, fails its own eval with a line that names the call. Two old bugs were found on the way: ids sometimes came out a character short, and the coverage fixture had never reached git.

Done whenarchitect prove --env local prints every eval ✔ and prove ✔ on acme; each planted fixture makes prove exit 1 and names the call on its own eval.

L1.13 The generators done

One command now turns acme's blueprint into a working codebase: the API contract, the server, a typed client and a command line, plus a starter handler for each operation that is written once and then left alone. // Generated from blueprint/things/allowance/verbs/grant.ts. Do not edit.
import { HttpApiEndpoint } from "effect/http-api";
import declaration from "../../../../blueprint/things/allowance/verbs/grant.ts";
import { AllowanceLimitTooHigh, NotAllowed } from "../errors.ts";

export { declaration };
export const endpoint = HttpApiEndpoint.post("grant", "/allowance/grant", {
  payload: declaration.payloadSchema,
  success: declaration.answerSchema,
  error: [AllowanceLimitTooHigh, NotAllowed],
});
export const steps = ["allowance.grant.insert"] as const;

// Scaffolded from blueprint/things/allowance/verbs/grant.ts. Yours from now on.
import { Effect } from "effect";
import { Handlers } from "@architect/target-effect";
import type { Handler } from "../../../generated/api/src/allowance/grant.handler.ts";

/** Let a member spend from the organisation, up to a limit each month. */
const grant: Handler = (_payload, _context) =>
  Effect.gen(function* () {
    // Payload: organization, member, limit.
    // Answer: allowance.
    // Steps, each its own span: steps.insert(...).
    // May refuse with AllowanceLimitTooHigh.
    return yield* Handlers.blank("this handler is not written yet");
  });

export default grant;

Stage 1 · the skeleton, with every eval

L1.12 The runtime done

Every operation now runs through one wrapper. It checks the payload, runs the handler as counted work so the world can tell when it's quiet, records a span named after the operation with the payload on it, gives each step its own span, and checks the answer. It runs inside real workerd, writing to a real local D1. Along the way, amounts now travel as text, since a JSON number can't hold 128 bits.

L1.11 The compiler done

Stage 3 · shipping on local worlds (Gate 2)

  
Build-integrity Immutable clean bundles and signed proof hashes done

  
Deployable builds refuse dirty source, retain a versioned clean-commit manifest and reuse an existing verified artifact without replacing it. Test and staging proofs sign the actual bundle hash; production carries that exact hash. The newest verdict must pass and name the same bytes. The logbook adds a nullable hash column in place, preserving all legacy rows and mixed-version reads/writes. Hashless legacy verdicts remain visible but cannot open a promotion gate.

  
Done whenarchitect promote --to production refuses a revision without staging verdicts and accepts one that has them. A dirty build followed by restoring source cannot replace the clean artifact. A newest signed staging verdict with another hash or no hash refuses production; matching hash-bound verdicts move exactly the staged bytes, and production carries that hash. Legacy cache provenance is refused, then a real clean bundle rebuild succeeds after preserving that cache aside. A real workerd logbook upgrades its own legacy persisted SQLite schema without losing rows or releases, keeps old hashless rows readable, rejects malformed hashes, round-trips new signed hashes, and restarts through idempotent initialization.

  
CI success · commit 53d028d · commit 8f50320 · 13 verified captures · 0 unverified records
